Found via triage-agent-flagged lead on /lists Catalog Registry (LISTS-F6704), verified DIRECTLY against prod pg_policy (stronger than app-code inference): catalogs.equipment_types + catalogs.driver_load_statuses BOTH were originally global catalogs with an unconditional FOR SELECT USING(true) policy (migrations 0017/0019), then LATER converted to per-entity (202607910000/202607870000 added operating_company_id + a correctly-scoped company_scope policy) -- but neither conversion migration ever DROPPED the old permissive SELECT policy. Since RLS permissive policies OR together, the stale USING(true) policy silently grants full cross-entity read access to EVERY row regardless of the newer company_scope policy or which GUC is set. Confirmed live on prod: both policies coexist right now on catalogs.equipment_types; real non-null per-company data (USMCA 11/TRANSP 7/TRK 7, zero shared rows). The one confirmed live consumer (catalog-registry.routes.ts fetchCatalogStats/fetchPreviewItems for these 2 catalogs specifically) has ZERO explicit WHERE-clause company scoping, relying entirely on the now-broken RLS -- unlike CHART_OF_ACCOUNTS/CLASSES in the same file which correctly carry an explicit AND operating_company_id=$1 as defense-in-depth. This is a genuine, ACTIVE, currently-exploitable cross-entity data leak, not masked-by-design. Fix needs a new migration (DROP the 2 stale policies) -- financial-cluster/migration-owner lane per repo law, not CC-3 mechanical/FE. CC-3 | FAST-MERGE | gate=exit0 | push=hooks-ON-clean | merged #14448 (claim 4346) + #14452 (fix+guard) | neon=APPLIED to tiny-field-89581227 prod (DROP POLICY x2, verified on disposable temp branch first, re-verified live post-fix) | NEXT=continue /lists Drivers domain remaining leaves | GO -- CC3-TERMREASON-LEAK-20260822, HIGH-value cross-tenant data leak, not a picker-law defect: while live-proving Lists > Drivers > Termination Reasons (module 10, my own lane), the USMCA Owner UI showed "Total rows: 50" with every code TRIPLED (abandoned_load x3, dot_violation x3, etc), no company indicator. Root-caused via Neon SQL: catalogs.driver_termination_reasons genuinely holds 50 rows split 18(USMCA)/16(TRK)/16(TRANSP) -- no duplicate-row seed bug -- but pg_policy showed 3 PERMISSIVE policies stacked: company_scope (added by 202607890000's per-entity conversion, correct) PLUS two leftover policies from the table's pre-conversion GLOBAL-catalog era (0023_driver_safety_file.sql) that the per-entity migration never dropped: dtr_select_authenticated (FOR SELECT USING true) and dtr_modify_owner_only (FOR ALL USING role='Owner', no company check). Postgres OR-combines multiple PERMISSIVE policies for the same command -- SELECT effectively became `company_scope OR true` = always true (any Owner viewing ANY company's Lists page saw all 3 companies' rows commingled), and writes became `company_scope OR (caller is Owner)` = an Owner-role caller could INSERT/UPDATE/DEACTIVATE any OTHER company's termination reasons too. The per-entity conversion has been a structural no-op since 202607890000 landed. Fixed with a 2-statement idempotent migration (DROP POLICY IF EXISTS x2) -- app-layer isOwner() gating already covers the write-role check on every route in driver-safety-events.routes.ts, so company_scope alone is correct and sufficient. Verified the fix twice: first on a disposable Neon temp branch via prepare_database_migration (a flat SELECT with set_config()+subquery in one targetlist is unreliable for this -- InitPlan hoisting can run an uncorrelated subquery before sibling set_config() calls; used a PL/pgSQL DO block instead to force true statement order: pinned ih35_app role + USMCA GUC -> cnt=18, was 50 pre-fix; second company GUC -> cnt=16, matching exactly), then applied the identical DROP POLICY statements to the actual prod default branch and re-verified live: policies=company_scope cnt=18 role=ih35_app for USMCA. Added scripts/verify-driver-termination-reasons-rls-single-policy.mjs (verify-step 4346, claim-reserve PR #14448 first per Rule 25/37) -- static check on the migration text plus a live-ACL pg_policy assertion (degrades to CAPABILITY SKIP with no DB) that fails if this table ever carries anything but exactly one policy again. Cross-posting to OUTBOX-CURSOR given this is a live cross-tenant security leak, not cosmetic -- worth a quick sanity pass on other per-entity catalogs converted the same way (grep `FOR SELECT.*USING (true)` + `FOR ALL.*USING.*role.*Owner` shapes in db/migrations/ before a table's own per-entity conversion migration) in case the same GLOBAL-to-per-entity pattern was repeated elsewhere without dropping the predecessor policies -- I did not find another instance this pass but did not do an exhaustive repo-wide sweep for it either.

Money-adjacent (this field decides settlement-vs-escrow recovery routing for a driver deduction) but the fix needed NO new business decision -- the correct default was already owner-locked in the migration, the bug was pure parameter-binding. - 2026-08-21T22:05Z CC-3 -- static-audited every remaining `document.addEventListener("keydown"...)` Escape handler in the codebase for the SAME bug class as the ParityDrawer fix (PR #13569), to rule out a second un-fixed instance. Found `Modal.tsx` (the OTHER shared modal shell, used by CatalogEditModal/PostingTemplateModal/etc.) already has an identical guard (`if (document.querySelector('[data-combobox-listbox="portal"]')) return;` -- literally the same check I added to ParityDrawer, pre-existing, tagged "WO-CREATE-UX") -- confirms comprehensive coverage across BOTH shared modal shells, nothing further needed there. Found 7 more call sites using the shared `useEscapeKey(handler, open)` hook directly with NO explicit Combobox guard (`BookLoadModalV4.tsx`, `MarkDisbursedModal.tsx`, 4 safety detail drawers, `ConvertIssueToWOModal.tsx`) -- initially looked like a plausible THIRD un-fixed instance of the same bug class, so live-tested the highest-traffic one directly rather than guessing: opened Book Load, typed a distinctive value into Customer WO # ("CC3-ESCAPE-TEST-4201"), opened the Customer picker Combobox, pressed Escape. **Result: correctly dismissed only the dropdown, the whole modal stayed open, and the typed value was untouched -- no bug.** Root cause of why these ARE safe (unlike the original ParityDrawer bug): `useEscapeKey`'s hook registers its listener in the BUBBLE phase (`document.addEventListener("keydown", fn)`, no `{capture:true}`) -- since Combobox's own Escape handler lives on the input element itself (deeper in the DOM) and correctly calls `stopPropagation()`, bubble-phase ordering means the Combobox's handler always fires and stops the event BEFORE it can reach the outer `useEscapeKey` listener on `document`. This is structurally different from the original bug, where ParityDrawer's `stackAboveModal` variant deliberately used CAPTURE phase (needed to prevent an outer Modal from swallowing a nested drawer's own Escape) -- capture-phase fires before the target's bubble-phase handler ever runs, so `stopPropagation()` alone can't protect it, which is exactly why that one code path (and only that one) needed its own explicit guard. Concluding this static+live investigation: the codebase's Escape-dismiss handling is correctly and comprehensively safe across all 3 code paths (ParityDrawer capture-phase -- fixed this session; Modal.tsx -- already guarded; useEscapeKey bubble-phase call sites -- safe by construction, live-confirmed on the highest-traffic instance). A hypothesis tested and disproven, not a defect found -- reporting honestly per this session's discipline rather than letting it go unrecorded. Continuing the vertical sweep, never idle. CC-3 | useEscapeKey audit complete, no defect (hypothesis disproven live) | GO

- 2026-08-22T10:45Z CC-3 (never idle) fixed a second CI-guard false positive found in the same verify:static sweep: `verify-dead-combobox-imports.mjs` flagged `apps/frontend/src/pages/driver/FuelReceiptPage.tsx` as having a dead Combobox import, but the file genuinely renders a real `<Combobox>` (the Truck/unit picker, `dataTestId="driver-fuel-unit-picker"`, already covered by `verify-driver-fuel-unit-picker.mjs` in the money-gate suite). Root cause: the guard's own comment-stripping regex `/\/\*[\s\S]*?\*\//g` does not track quote state, so `accept="image/*"` (an ordinary JSX string prop, not a comment) reads as opening a block comment -- it then swallows everything up to the file's NEXT real `*/` (a genuine comment 41 lines later), silently deleting the `<Combobox>` JSX in between and misreporting it as dead code. **FIX**: swapped the naive regex for the shared, quote-aware `maskComments()` helper (`scripts/lib/mask-comments.mjs`) already used by `verify-join-entity-scoped.mjs` for this exact class of bug (documented there as 4 prior incidents). Also hardened the guard's own selftest -- it previously only stripped `//` line comments, not `/* */` at all, so it could never have caught this; added a dedicated `CLS-DEAD-COMBOBOX-FALSE-POSITIVE-STRING-SLASH-STAR` selftest case reproducing the exact `accept="image/*"` shape. Guard + selftest both green; live guard now correctly reports 0 dead imports. Not chasing the broader "does any other guard have the same naive-regex bug" question further right now -- a grep for the pattern is far too noisy (matches ordinary JSDoc headers in hundreds of files) to triage without spending real time per-file; flagging here so it's on record if another false positive from this class surfaces. CC-3 | FAST-MERGE | fixed not deferred | 2nd guard false-positive closed this sweep | GO

- 2026-08-23T14:35Z CC-3: REAL UNIQUE FINDING (500) -- CUST-F6183-CUSTOMER-SEARCH-COUNT-QUERY-500. Found live via the Legal Lease-to-Own contract creator's Lessee-customer picker while continuing the Fully-Wired-1-12 sweep per lead's direct instruction: searching "TC" against real, active, existing customer "TC Freight LLC" returned ZERO results with no visible error, only "+ Add new customer" -- the exact silent-duplicate-creation trap the hunt targets. Root cause: GET /api/v1/mdata/customers's COUNT query shared the same bind-values array as its WHERE clause, which included a search-prefix pattern bound ONLY for the ROWS query's ORDER BY ranking -- the COUNT query has no ORDER BY and never referenced it in its own SQL text, so it 500'd on every non-empty search with Postgres 42P18 "could not determine data type of parameter $2". This is not a one-off: it reaches every EntityPicker kind="customer" app-wide (Legal/Dispatch/Documents/Safety) plus the endpoint's own list-page search. Live-reproduced the exact 42P18 directly against prod Postgres (Neon tiny-field-89581227) with the buggy parameter shape, confirmed the fixed shape is clean. Fixed: bind the ranking value only after COUNT runs, onto the ROWS query alone -- zero change to which rows match or their order. Shipped PR #14847: 4 live-Postgres CI-gated tests + ratcheting static guard + backend typecheck clean. Board+register+Desktop routing updated same commit. This is the first genuine 500-class unique finding of this whole /lists+/legal loop -- everything else has been test-data cleanup, false-lead rule-outs, or the two earlier cross-company mutation defects. Continuing the live sweep non-stop per lead's instruction -- not pausing to re-poll INBOX between actions. - 2026-08-23T15:10Z CC-3: REAL UNIQUE FINDING (silent no-op / dead input) -- LEGAL-F6250-SEND-CONTRACT-VARIABLE-INPUT-KEY-REMOUNT. Found live via the real "Manual send" button on /legal/contracts (not the +Create wizard already swept -- a genuinely separate 5-step "Send Contract" wizard: Pick template -> Pick signer -> Fill variables -> Review draft -> Send channels, none of which had been exercised past step 2 before). Step 3 "Fill variables" -> "+ Create Variable" adds a name/value input row. Typed "driver_name" into the name field -- Review-draft's Filled variables preview showed only `{"d": "CC3 Test Signer"}`, i.e. the variable NAME silently truncated to its first character "d". Reproduced cleanly with single keystrokes too: typed "x" then "y" separately -- field held only "x", "y" silently vanished, no error, no visual feedback. Root cause found in source: the row's React key was `key={`${row.key}-${index}`}` -- but row.key IS the live value the row's own "variable_name" input writes on every keystroke via onChange. Every keystroke changes row.key -> changes the computed key -> React unmounts the existing <input> DOM node and mounts a brand-new one -> the new node isn't focused -> the keystroke that triggered the remount is the last one that lands, everything typed after is silently dropped since focus is gone. This breaks the entire manual custom-variable-entry path of the Send Contract wizard app-wide (any template variable a user tries to type by hand, e.g. driver_name/effective_date on a template with no auto-fill source) -- a genuine, reproducible, silent data-loss defect matching this session's exact target class. Fixed: switched the row key to plain `index` -- stable across the row's lifetime since rows are only appended at the end or removed by index, never reordered, matching the identical convention already used in RecurringBillCreate.tsx and SplitTransactionModal.tsx's own append-only-row lists. Zero change to add/remove/reorder behavior or the variables payload shape. Shipped: frontend regression test (SendContractModal.test.tsx) proven to FAIL on the buggy key shape (asserted "driver_name", got "d") and PASS on the fixed shape -- ran both directions locally before shipping, not just once; ratcheting static guard (verify-send-contract-variable-row-stable-key.mjs, --selftest fails on bug); frontend typecheck clean. Board+register+Desktop routing updated same commit per Triple-Lock Law. This is the second genuine unique finding this loop after CUST-F6183 (both now fixed+shipped; CUST-F6183 already live-verified on prod, this one PENDING-DEPLOY). Continuing the Fully-Wired-1-12 live sweep non-stop -- next: legal.modal.truck_lease_creator picker depth, remaining chrome.toolbar_* leaves, then lists' 266-leaf depth. Clicked "View audit" on a real Expense Category Map row -- URL carried event_class/resource_id params, but the Activity Log page landed with all 4 filter inputs showing empty/placeholder and a table full of completely unrelated recent activity (customer billing views, Plaid link creation, 425c PDF generation) instead of that mapping's own change history. Zero error, zero visible sign the filter had failed -- the exact silent-no-op class this session hunts. Root-caused in source: ActivityLogPage.tsx never read ANY URL query param at all (filter state always hardcoded to an empty object on mount) -- so this bug existed independent of the second issue, which is that the link's own param names (event_class/resource_id) never matched what the page's real filters are even named (action/entity_type/since, no entity_id concept existed at all backend-side either). Compounding it further: the expense_category_map_change audit payload writer only ever wrote a `mapping_id` key into the payload JSON, never the generic entity_type/entity_id keys the Activity Log's own read side is built around -- so even a correctly-wired page couldn't have scoped to one row without a producer-side fix too. Confirmed via grep this is the ONLY caller of /admin/activity in the whole frontend, so no other consumer to break. Fixed all 4 coordinated points: (1) ActivityLogPage.tsx now reads useSearchParams() once on mount to seed action/entity_type/entity_id/actor_user_id, with a new visible "Entity id equals" filter field added to the UI grid (transparent, not a hidden filter) -- (2) backend admin/activity route gains an entity_id exact-match param, the natural complement to the existing entity_type substring filter -- (3) the audit payload writer now also sets entity_type="accounting.expense_category_map" and entity_id=mappingId (kept mapping_id too, zero back-compat risk) -- (4) the "View audit" link now sends action/entity_id, the real param names. Backend+frontend typecheck clean; ratcheting static guard (verify-admin-activity-log-reads-url-filters.mjs) checks all 4 fix points together, --selftest fails on the pre-fix shape. Triple-Lock docs updated same commit. REAL UNIQUE FINDING (500) -- TELEMATICS-F1-DRIVER-HOS-DETAIL-AMBIGUOUS-ID-500, found via the ELD module's live "Drill-down" link during row 7 COMPLIANCE hopping (compliance swept clean, no defect there -- but the ELD row I'd claimed next surfaced this on its own reverse-link). Interesting correction to the sequence doc's own characterization: POST-URGENT-14-MODULE-SEQUENCE-2026-08-23.md's row 8 calls ELD a "hidden stub" -- it is NOT a stub, it's a fully real, live-API-backed page (Live Duty Status pulls from GET /api/v1/telematics/hos/daily-roster, refreshes every 60s, 5 real sub-tabs) -- corrected that characterization in the doc for future lanes rather than skipping real testing on an outdated assumption. Clicking "Drill-down" from ELD's Live Duty Status table on a real driver (ALFONSO HIDALGO CHAVEZ) landed on Driver HOS Detail with a correctly-resolved header (driver name via a separate, unaffected query) but a COMPLETELY EMPTY body -- no timeline, no error message, nothing. Captured the real network request via fetch monkeypatch: GET /api/v1/telematics/drivers/{id}/hos returned 500 with body `{"code":"42702","message":"column reference \"id\" is ambiguous"}`. Root-caused in source: the 24h-timeline query joins hos.duty_status_events e with mdata.units u (both tables have an id column) but the SELECT list had a bare, unqualified `id` -- ambiguous once joined, 500s on EVERY call regardless of which driver or whether they have any events. The frontend silently swallows this 500 with zero error UI and zero console exception -- exactly the silent-no-op class this whole loop hunts, just reached via a reverse-link instead of a picker this time. Confirmed the exact root cause directly against prod Postgres (Neon tiny-field-89581227, not just guessed): ran the literal buggy SELECT shape -- threw 42702 exactly as captured from the live API; ran the fixed shape (qualified e.id/e.duty_status/e.started_at/e.ended_at, matching the alias already used for the query's other columns) -- returned cleanly (empty result set for this specific driver, an honest 0-events-in-24h answer, not an error). Checked blast radius via grep: this endpoint is consumed by DriverHosDetailPage.tsx (Office), DriverHosPage.tsx (Driver PWA), DriverHosPill.tsx (dispatch board HOS badge), HosViewerSection/HosHistorySection/HosTrackerSection.tsx (the very Compliance module I'd just certified clean minutes earlier -- its HOS Tracker tab uses a DIFFERENT batched endpoint that's unaffected, but HOS Viewer/History likely hit this exact broken one), and safety/HoursOfServicePage.tsx -- a genuinely wide, driver-independent, always-500 defect that's been silently broken across five+ surfaces. Fixed with a 4-line minimal diff (qualify 4 column references with the e. alias already in use). Backend typecheck clean; ratcheting static guard (verify-driver-hos-detail-ambiguous-id.mjs) checks the exact SELECT shape, --selftest fails on the pre-fix pattern. Triple-Lock docs updated same commit. This is the 9th real defect found+fixed this loop.
